It provides parsers for common file formats (MiniSEED, SAC, etc.), signal processing routines, and tools for working with seismic waveforms.\n\n## Installation\n```bash\npip install obspy\n```\n\n## Key Concepts\n\n### Stream and Trace\n- **Trace**: A single continuous time series of seismic data\n- **Stream**: A collection of Trace objects (typically multiple channels/stations)\n\n### Reading Waveform Data\n\n```python\nfrom obspy import read\n\n# Read MiniSEED file\nst = read('/path/to/data.mseed')\n\n# Stream contains multiple traces\nprint(st)  # Shows summary of all traces\n\n# Access individual traces\nfor tr in st:\n    print(f\"Station: {tr.stats.station}\")\n    print(f\"Channel: {tr.stats.channel}\")\n    print(f\"Network: {tr.stats.network}\")\n    print(f\"Start time: {tr.stats.starttime}\")\n    print(f\"End time: {tr.stats.endtime}\")\n    print(f\"Sampling rate: {tr.stats.sampling_rate}\")\n    print(f\"Number of samples: {tr.stats.npts}\")\n```\n\n### Trace Stats Object\nEach trace has a `stats` attribute with metadata:\n- `network`: Network code (e.g., \"CI\")\n- `station`: Station code (e.g., \"CCC\")\n- `location`: Location code\n- `channel`: Channel code (e.g., \"BHZ\")\n- `starttime`: UTCDateTime object for start\n- `endtime`: UTCDateTime object for end\n- `sampling_rate`: Samples per second\n- `delta`: Sample interval in seconds\n- `npts`: Number of points\n\n### Filtering Streams\n```python\n# Select by station\nst_station = st.select(station=\"CCC\")\n\n# Select by channel (e.g., all vertical components)\nst_vertical = st.select(channel=\"*Z\")\n\n# Select by network\nst_network = st.select(network=\"CI\")\n\n# Combine criteria\nst_filtered = st.select(network=\"CI\", station=\"CCC\", channel=\"BHZ\")\n```\n\n### Time Operations\n```python\nfrom obspy import UTCDateTime\n\n# Create time objects\nt = UTCDateTime(\"2019-07-04T17:33:49\")\nt = UTCDateTime(2019, 7, 4, 17, 33, 49)\n\n# Slice stream by time\nst_trimmed = st.slice(starttime=t, endtime=t + 60)\n\n# Trim in place\nst.trim(starttime=t, endtime=t + 60)\n```\n\n### Signal Processing\n```python\n# Remove mean\nst.detrend('demean')\n\n# Remove linear trend\nst.detrend('linear')\n\n# Filter (bandpass)\nst.filter('bandpass', freqmin=1.0, freqmax=10.0)\n\n# Taper edges\nst.taper(max_percentage=0.05)\n\n# Resample\nst.resample(100.0)  # 100 Hz\n```\n\n### Converting to NumPy\n```python\n# Get data as numpy array\ndata = tr.data  # Returns numpy array\n\n# Get times as numpy array\ntimes = tr.times()  # Seconds since start\ntimes_utc = tr.times(\"utcdatetime\")  # UTCDateTime objects\n```\n\n### Merging and Copying\n```python\n# Deep copy\nst_copy = st.copy()\n\n# Merge traces with same ID\nst.merge(method=1, fill_value='interpolate')\n```\n\n## Common Patterns\n\n### Group Traces by Station\n```python\nfrom collections import defaultdict\n\nstation_traces = defaultdict(list)\nfor tr in st:\n    key = f\"{tr.stats.network}.{tr.stats.station}\"\n    station_traces[key].append(tr)\n```\n\n### Create 3-Component Waveforms\n```python\ndef get_3c_waveform(st, network, station):\n    \"\"\"Get E, N, Z components for a station.\"\"\"\n    st_sta = st.select(network=network, station=station)\n\n    # {"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-5-20251101","id":"msg_vrtx_01R9B3SGAXDQSggTnFuYw87Y","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_vrtx_017b3TnP5QgyakJa9aXpHdqW","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/root/environment/skills/seismic-phase-association.md","content":"# Seismic Phase Association\n\n## Overview\nPhase association is the process of grouping seismic phase picks (P and S waves) from multiple stations that belong to the same earthquake event. This is a critical step between phase picking and earthquake location.\n\n## Key Concepts\n\n### The Association Problem\nWhen an earthquake occurs:\n1. Seismic waves travel outward from the source\n2. P-waves arrive first (faster, ~6 km/s)\n3. S-waves arrive later (slower, ~3.5 km/s)\n4. Different stations record arrivals at different times based on distance\n\nThe goal is to identify which picks belong to the same earthquake event.\n\n### Velocity Model\nA simple 1D velocity model:\n```python\nvp = 6.0  # P-wave velocity in km/s\nvs = vp / 1.75  # S-wave velocity (approximately 3.43 km/s)\n```\n\n### Distance Calculation\n```python\nfrom math import radians, sin, cos, sqrt, atan2\n\ndef haversine_distance(lat1, lon1, lat2, lon2):\n    \"\"\"Calculate distance between two points in km.\"\"\"\n    R = 6371  # Earth's radius in km\n\n    lat1, lon1, lat2, lon2 = map(radians, [lat1, lon1, lat2, lon2])\n    dlat = lat2 - lat1\n    dlon = lon2 - lon1\n\n    a = sin(dlat/2)**2 + cos(lat1) * cos(lat2) * sin(dlon/2)**2\n    c = 2 * atan2(sqrt(a), sqrt(1-a))\n\n    return R * c\n```\n\n## Association Methods\n\n### 1. DBSCAN-based Clustering\nCluster picks based on predicted origin time:\n```python\nfrom sklearn.cluster import DBSCAN\nimport numpy as np\n\ndef associate_dbscan(picks_df, stations_df, vp=6.0, vs=3.43, eps_seconds=2.0):\n    \"\"\"\n    Associate picks using DBSCAN clustering on predicted origin times.\n\n    For each pick, calculate the predicted origin time:\n    - P pick: origin_time = pick_time - distance/vp\n    - S pick: origin_time = pick_time - distance/vs\n\n    Picks from the same event should have similar predicted origin times.\n    \"\"\"\n    # Calculate origin time for each pick\n    origin_times = []\n    for _, pick in picks_df.iterrows():\n        station = stations_df[stations_df['station'] == pick['station']].iloc[0]\n        # Use network centroid as reference point\n        # In practice, iterate to find best location\n\n        # Estimate distance based on S-P time if available\n        velocity = vp if pick['phase'] == 'P' else vs\n        # Approximate: use relative timing\n        origin_times.append(pick['time'])\n\n    # Cluster picks with similar predicted origin times\n    times_array = np.array([t.timestamp for t in origin_times]).reshape(-1, 1)\n\n    clustering = DBSCAN(eps=eps_seconds, min_samples=3).fit(times_array)\n\n    return clustering.labels_\n```\n\n### 2. Grid Search Association\n```python\ndef grid_search_associate(picks_df, stations_df, vp=6.0, vs=3.43):\n    \"\"\"\n    Search over possible source locations and origin times.\n    Find combinations that minimize residuals.\n    \"\"\"\n    # Define search grid\n    lat_range = np.linspace(stations_df['latitude'].min(),\n                           stations_df['latitude'].max(), 20)\n    lon_range = np.linspace(stations_df['longitude'].min(),\n                           stations_df['longitude'].max(), 20)\n\n    events = []\n    used_picks = set()\n\n    for origin_time_guess in get_time_windows(picks_df):\n        best_fit = None\n        best_residual = float('inf')\n\n        for lat in lat_range:\n            for lon in lon_range:\n                # Calculate predicted arrivals\n                residuals = []\n                matching_picks = []\n\n                for idx, pick in picks_df.iterrows():\n                    if idx in used_picks:\n                        continue\n\n                    sta = stations_df[stations_df['station'] == pick['station']].iloc[0]\n                    dist = haversine_distance(lat, lon, sta['latitude'], sta['longitude'])\n\n                    v = vp if pick['phase'] == 'P' else vs\n                    predicted_time = origin_time_guess + dist / v\n\n                    residual = abs((pick['time'] - predicted_time).total_seconds())\n                    if residual < 2.0:  # 2 second tolerance\n                        residuals.append(residual)\n                        matching_picks.append(idx)\n\n                if len(matching_picks) >= 4:  # Minimum picks for event\n                    avg_residual = np.mean(residuals)\n                    if avg_residual < best_residual:\n                        best_residual = avg_residual\n                        best_fit = {\n                            'time': origin_time_guess,\n                            'lat': lat,\n                            'lon': lon,\n                            'picks': matching_picks\n                        }\n\n        if best_fit:\n            events.append(best_fit)\n            used_picks.update(best_fit['picks'])\n\n    return events\n```\n\n### 3. GaMMA Association (Modern Approach)\nGaMMA is a Gaussian Mixture Model Associator designed for seismic data:\n```python\n# pip install gamma-seismic\nfrom gamma import BayesianGaussianMixture\n\n# Prepare picks for GaMMA\npicks_gamma = picks_df[['time', 'station', 'phase', 'probability']].copy()\nstations_gamma = stations_df[['station', 'longitude', 'latitude', 'elevation_m']].copy()\n\n# Run association\ncatalog = BayesianGaussianMixture(\n    picks=picks_gamma,\n    stations=stations_gamma,\n    use_amplitude=False,\n    vel={\"p\": 6.0, \"s\": 3.43}\n).fit()\n```\n\n### 4. Simple Time-Window Association\n```python\ndef simple_associate(picks_df, window_seconds=30):\n    \"\"\"\n    Simple association based on time windows.\n    Groups picks that occur within a time window.\n    \"\"\"\n    picks_sorted = picks_df.sort_values('time')\n    events = []\n    current_event = []\n    current_start = None\n\n    for idx, pick in picks_sorted.iterrows():\n        if current_start is None:\n            current_start = pick['time']\n            current_event = [pick]\n        elif (pick['time'] - current_start).total_seconds() < window_seconds:\n            current_event.append(pick)\n        else:\n            if len(current_event) >= 3:  # Minimum stations\n                events.append({\n                    'time': current_event[0]['time'],\n                    'picks': current_event\n                })\n            current_start = pick['time']\n            current_event = [pick]\n\n    # Don't forget last event\n    if len(current_event) >= 3:\n        events.append({\n            'time': current_event[0]['time'],\n            'picks': current_event\n        })\n\n    return events\n```\n\n## Estimating Event Origin Time\n\n### From S-P Time Difference\n```python\ndef estimate_origin_time(p_time, s_time, vp=6.0, vs=3.43):\n    \"\"\"\n    Estimate origin time and distance from S-P time.\n\n    ts - tp = d/vs - d/vp = d * (1/vs - 1/vp)\n    d = (ts - tp) / (1/vs - 1/vp)\n    origin_time = tp - d/vp\n    \"\"\"\n    sp_diff = (s_time - p_time).total_seconds()\n    distance = sp_diff / (1/vs - 1/vp)\n    origin_time = p_time - distance / vp\n    return origin_time, distance\n```\n\n### From Multiple Stations\n```python\ndef refine_origin_time(picks, stations_df, vp=6.0, vs=3.43, initial_lat=None, initial_lon=None):\n    \"\"\"\n    Refine origin time using multiple stations.\n    Minimize travel time residuals.\n    \"\"\"\n    from scipy.optimize import minimize\n\n    def objective(params):\n        origin_time_offset, lat, lon = params\n        base_time = picks.iloc[0]['time']\n        origin_time = base_time + timedelta(seconds=origin_time_offset)\n\n        residuals = []\n        for _, pick in picks.iterrows():\n            sta = stations_df[stations_df['station'] == pick['station']].iloc[0]\n            dist = haversine_distance(lat, lon, sta['latitude'], sta['longitude'])\n\n            v = vp if pick['phase'] == 'P' else vs\n            predicted_arrival = origin_time + timedelta(seconds=dist/v)\n\n            residual = (pick['time'] - predicted_arrival).total_seconds()\n            residuals.append(residual ** 2)\n\n        return np.sum(residuals)\n\n    # Initial guess\n    x0 = [-10, initial_lat or 35.7, initial_lon or -117.5]\n\n    result = minimize(objective, x0, method='Nelder-Mead')\n\n    origin_time_offset = result.x[0]\n    origin_time = picks.iloc[0]['time'] + timedelta(seconds=origin_time_offset)\n\n    return origin_time, result.x[1], result.x[2]\n```\n\n## Best Practices\n\n1. **Quality Control**: Filter picks by probability threshold (e.g., > 0.3)\n2. **Minimum Picks**: Require at least 3-4 picks per event\n3. **P-S Consistency**: Check that S arrivals follow P arrivals at same station\n4. **Travel Time Consistency**: Verify picks are consistent with velocity model\n5. **Deduplication**: Merge events within a few seconds of each other\n"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":13,"cache_creation_input_tokens":1327,"cache_read_input_tokens":25693,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":1327,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":18},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"1eeb155e-f9ee-4015-9075-691a021ae072","uuid":"11635eb3-3214-455e-b39b-774738a5746f"}

Seismic waves travel outward from the source\n2. P-waves arrive first (faster, ~6 km/s)\n3. S-waves arrive later (slower, ~3.5 km/s)\n4. Different stations record arrivals at different times based on distance\n\nThe goal is to identify which picks belong to the same earthquake event.\n\n### Velocity Model\nA simple 1D velocity model:\n```python\nvp = 6.0  # P-wave velocity in km/s\nvs = vp / 1.75  # S-wave velocity (approximately 3.43 km/s)\n```\n\n### Distance Calculation\n```python\nfrom math import radians, sin, cos, sqrt, atan2\n\ndef haversine_distance(lat1, lon1, lat2, lon2):\n    \"\"\"Calculate distance between two points in km.\"\"\"\n    R = 6371  # Earth's radius in km\n\n    lat1, lon1, lat2, lon2 = map(radians, [lat1, lon1, lat2, lon2])\n    dlat = lat2 - lat1\n    dlon = lon2 - lon1\n\n    a = sin(dlat/2)**2 + cos(lat1) * cos(lat2) * sin(dlon/2)**2\n    c = 2 * atan2(sqrt(a), sqrt(1-a))\n\n    return R * c\n```\n\n## Association Methods\n\n### 1. DBSCAN-based Clustering\nCluster picks based on predicted origin time:\n```python\nfrom sklearn.cluster import DBSCAN\nimport numpy as np\n\ndef associate_dbscan(picks_df, stations_df, vp=6.0, vs=3.43, eps_seconds=2.0):\n    \"\"\"\n    Associate picks using DBSCAN clustering on predicted origin times.\n\n    For each pick, calculate the predicted origin time:\n    - P pick: origin_time = pick_time - distance/vp\n    - S pick: origin_time = pick_time - distance/vs\n\n    Picks from the same event should have similar predicted origin times.\n    \"\"\"\n    # Calculate origin time for each pick\n    origin_times = []\n    for _, pick in picks_df.iterrows():\n        station = stations_df[stations_df['station'] == pick['station']].iloc[0]\n        # Use network centroid as reference point\n        # In practice, iterate to find best location\n\n        # Estimate distance based on S-P time if available\n        velocity = vp if pick['phase'] == 'P' else vs\n        # Approximate: use relative timing\n        origin_times.append(pick['time'])\n\n    # Cluster picks with similar predicted origin times\n    times_array = np.array([t.timestamp for t in origin_times]).reshape(-1, 1)\n\n    clustering = DBSCAN(eps=eps_seconds, min_samples=3).fit(times_array)\n\n    return clustering.labels_\n```\n\n### 2. Grid Search Association\n```python\ndef grid_search_associate(picks_df, stations_df, vp=6.0, vs=3.43):\n    \"\"\"\n    Search over possible source locations and origin times.\n    Find combinations that minimize residuals.\n    \"\"\"\n    # Define search grid\n    lat_range = np.linspace(stations_df['latitude'].min(),\n                           stations_df['latitude'].max(), 20)\n    lon_range = np.linspace(stations_df['longitude'].min(),\n                           stations_df['longitude'].max(), 20)\n\n    events = []\n    used_picks = set()\n\n    for origin_time_guess in get_time_windows(picks_df):\n        best_fit = None\n        best_residual = float('inf')\n\n        for lat in lat_range:\n            for lon in lon_range:\n                # Calculate predicted arrivals\n                residuals = []\n                matching_picks = []\n\n                for idx, pick in picks_df.iterrows():\n                    if idx in used_picks:\n                        continue\n\n                    sta = stations_df[stations_df['station'] == pick['station']].iloc[0]\n                    dist = haversine_distance(lat, lon, sta['latitude'], sta['longitude'])\n\n                    v = vp if pick['phase'] == 'P' else vs\n                    predicted_time = origin_time_guess + dist / v\n\n                    residual = abs((pick['time'] - predicted_time).total_seconds())\n                    if residual < 2.0:  # 2 second tolerance\n                        residuals.append(residual)\n                        matching_picks.append(idx)\n\n                if len(matching_picks) >= 4:  # Minimum picks for event\n                    avg_residual = np.mean(residuals)\n                    if avg_residual < best_residual:\n                        best_residual = avg_residual\n                        best_fit = {\n                            'time': origin_time_guess,\n                            'lat': lat,\n                            'lon': lon,\n                            'picks': matching_picks\n                        }\n\n        if best_fit:\n            events.append(best_fit)\n            used_picks.update(best_fit['picks'])\n\n    return events\n```\n\n### 3. GaMMA Association (Modern Approach)\nGaMMA is a Gaussian Mixture Model Associator designed for seismic data:\n```python\n# pip install gamma-seismic\nfrom gamma import BayesianGaussianMixture\n\n# Prepare picks for GaMMA\npicks_gamma = picks_df[['time', 'station', 'phase', 'probability']].copy()\nstations_gamma = stations_df[['station', 'longitude', 'latitude', 'elevation_m']].copy()\n\n# Run association\ncatalog = BayesianGaussianMixture(\n    picks=picks_gamma,\n    stations=stations_gamma,\n    use_amplitude=False,\n    vel={\"p\": 6.0, \"s\": 3.43}\n).fit()\n```\n\n### 4. Simple Time-Window Association\n```python\ndef simple_associate(picks_df, window_seconds=30):\n    \"\"\"\n    Simple association based on time windows.\n    Groups picks that occur within a time window.\n    \"\"\"\n    picks_sorted = picks_df.sort_values('time')\n    events = []\n    current_event = []\n    current_start = None\n\n    for idx, pick in picks_sorted.iterrows():\n        if current_start is None:\n            current_start = pick['time']\n            current_event = [pick]\n        elif (pick['time'] - current_start).total_seconds() < window_seconds:\n            current_event.append(pick)\n        else:\n            if len(current_event) >= 3:  # Minimum stations\n                events.append({\n                    'time': current_event[0]['time'],\n                    'picks': current_event\n                })\n            current_start = pick['time']\n            current_event = [pick]\n\n    # Don't forget last event\n    if len(current_event) >= 3:\n        events.append({\n            'time': current_event[0]['time'],\n            'picks': current_event\n        })\n\n    return events\n```\n\n## Estimating Event Origin Time\n\n### From S-P Time Difference\n```python\ndef estimate_origin_time(p_time, s_time, vp=6.0, vs=3.43):\n    \"\"\"\n    Estimate origin time and distance from S-P time.\n\n    ts - tp = d/vs - d/vp = d * (1/vs - 1/vp)\n    d = (ts - tp) / (1/vs - 1/vp)\n    origin_time = tp - d/vp\n    \"\"\"\n    sp_diff = (s_time - p_time).total_seconds()\n    distance = sp_diff / (1/vs - 1/vp)\n    origin_time = p_time - distance / vp\n    return origin_time, distance\n```\n\n### From Multiple Stations\n```python\ndef refine_origin_time(picks, stations_df, vp=6.0, vs=3.43, initial_lat=None, initial_lon=None):\n    \"\"\"\n    Refine origin time using multiple stations.\n    Minimize travel time residuals.\n    \"\"\"\n    from scipy.optimize import minimize\n\n    def objective(params):\n        origin_time_offset, lat, lon = params\n        base_time = picks.iloc[0]['time']\n        origin_time = base_time + timedelta(seconds=origin_time_offset)\n\n        residuals = []\n        for _, pick in picks.iterrows():\n            sta = stations_df[stations_df['station'] == pick['station']].iloc[0]\n            dist = haversine_distance(lat, lon, sta['latitude'], sta['longitude'])\n\n            v = vp if pick['phase'] == 'P' else vs\n            predicted_arrival = origin_time + timedelta(seconds=dist/v)\n\n            residual = (pick['time'] - predicted_arrival).total_seconds()\n            residuals.append(residual ** 2)\n\n        return np.sum(residuals)\n\n    # Initial guess\n    x0 = [-10, initial_lat or 35.7, initial_lon or -117.5]\n\n    result = minimize(objective, x0, method='Nelder-Mead')\n\n    origin_time_offset = result.x[0]\n    origin_time = picks.iloc[0]['time'] + timedelta(seconds=origin_time_offset)\n\n    return origin_time, result.x[1], result.x[2]\n```\n\n## Best Practices\n\n1. **Quality Control**: Filter picks by probability threshold (e.g., > 0.3)\n2. **Minimum Picks**: Require at least 3-4 picks per event\n3. **P-S Consistency**: Check that S arrivals follow P arrivals at same station\n4. **Travel Time Consistency**: Verify picks are consistent with velocity model\n5. **Deduplication**: Merge events within a few seconds of each other\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null}}

Calculate reference point (centroid of stations)\n    ref_lat = stations_df['latitude'].mean()\n    ref_lon = stations_df['longitude'].mean()\n\n    # For each pick, estimate origin time assuming source at reference point\n    origin_times = []\n    valid_picks = []\n\n    for idx, pick in picks_df.iterrows():\n        sta_match = stations_df[stations_df['station'] == pick['station']]\n        if len(sta_match) == 0:\n            continue\n\n        sta = sta_match.iloc[0]\n        dist = haversine_distance(ref_lat, ref_lon, sta['latitude'], sta['longitude'])\n\n        v = vp if pick['phase'] == 'P' else vs\n        travel_time = dist / v\n\n        origin_time = pick['time'] - timedelta(seconds=travel_time)\n        origin_times.append(origin_time.timestamp())\n        valid_picks.append(pick)\n\n    if len(origin_times) < min_samples:\n        return []\n\n    # Cluster\n    X = np.array(origin_times).reshape(-1, 1)\n    clustering = DBSCAN(eps=eps, min_samples=min_samples).fit(X)\n\n    # Group picks by cluster\n    events = []\n    for label in set(clustering.labels_):\n        if label == -1:  # Noise\n            continue\n\n        event_picks = [valid_picks[i] for i in range(len(valid_picks))\n                      if clustering.labels_[i] == label]\n\n        # Use earliest P pick as approximate origin time\n        p_picks = [p for p in event_picks if p['phase'] == 'P']\n        if p_picks:\n            first_p = min(p_picks, key=lambda x: x['time'])\n            sta = stations_df[stations_df['station'] == first_p['station']].iloc[0]\n            dist = haversine_distance(ref_lat, ref_lon, sta['latitude'], sta['longitude'])\n            origin_time = first_p['time'] - timedelta(seconds=dist/vp)\n        else:\n            # Use S picks if no P picks\n            first_pick = min(event_picks, key=lambda x: x['time'])\n            sta = stations_df[stations_df['station'] == first_pick['station']].iloc[0]\n            dist = haversine_distance(ref_lat, ref_lon, sta['latitude'], sta['longitude'])\n            origin_time = first_pick['time'] - timedelta(seconds=dist/vs)\n\n        events.append({\n            'time': origin_time,\n            'num_picks': len(event_picks),\n            'stations': list(set([p['station'] for p in event_picks]))\n        })\n\n    return events\n\n\ndef haversine_distance(lat1, lon1, lat2, lon2):\n    \"\"\"Calculate distance in km between two geographic points.\"\"\"\n    from math import radians, sin, cos, sqrt, atan2\n    R = 6371  # Geospatial Distance Calculations\n\n## Overview\nCalculating distances between geographic coordinates is essential for seismic analysis, as travel times depend on the distance between earthquake source and recording stations.\n\n## Haversine Formula\n\nThe Haversine formula calculates the great-circle distance between two points on a sphere.\n\n```python\nfrom math import radians, sin, cos, sqrt, atan2\n\ndef haversine_distance(lat1: float, lon1: float, lat2: float, lon2: float) -> float:\n    \"\"\"\n    Calculate the great-circle distance between two points on Earth.\n\n    Args:\n        lat1, lon1: First point coordinates in degrees\n        lat2, lon2: Second point coordinates in degrees\n\n    Returns:\n        Distance in kilometers\n    \"\"\"\n    R = 6371  # Earth's mean radius in km\n\n    # Convert to radians\n    lat1, lon1, lat2, lon2 = map(radians, [lat1, lon1, lat2, lon2])\n\n    # Differences\n    dlat = lat2 - lat1\n    dlon = lon2 - lon1\n\n    # Haversine formula\n    a = sin(dlat/2)**2 + cos(lat1) * cos(lat2) * sin(dlon/2)**2\n    c = 2 * atan2(sqrt(a), sqrt(1-a))\n\n    return R * c\n```\n\n## Vectorized Distance Calculation with NumPy\n\n```python\nimport numpy as np\n\ndef haversine_vectorized(lat1, lon1, lat2, lon2):\n    \"\"\"\n    Vectorized haversine distance calculation.\n\n    All inputs can be scalars or arrays.\n    Returns distance in kilometers.\n    \"\"\"\n    R = 6371\n\n    lat1, lon1, lat2, lon2 = map(np.radians, [lat1, lon1, lat2, lon2])\n\n    dlat = lat2 - lat1\n    dlon = lon2 - lon1\n\n    a = np.sin(dlat/2)**2 + np.cos(lat1) * np.cos(lat2) * np.sin(dlon/2)**2\n    c = 2 * np.arctan2(np.sqrt(a), np.sqrt(1-a))\n\n    return R * c\n```\n\n## Using GeoPy\n\n```python\nfrom geopy.distance import geodesic\n\n# More accurate ellipsoidal distance\npoint1 = (35.5, -117.5)  # (latitude, longitude)\npoint2 = (35.7, -117.3)\n\ndistance_km = geodesic(point1, point2).kilometers\ndistance_m = geodesic(point1, point2).meters\n```\n\n## Distance Matrix\n\nCalculate distances from a point to all stations:\n\n```python\nimport pandas as pd\nimport numpy as np\n\ndef calculate_distances_to_stations(source_lat, source_lon, stations_df):\n    \"\"\"\n    Calculate distance from a source point to all stations.\n\n    Args:\n        source_lat, source_lon: Source coordinates in degrees\n        stations_df: DataFrame with 'latitude' and 'longitude' columns\n\n    Returns:\n        Array of distances in km\n    \"\"\"\n    distances = haversine_vectorized(\n        source_lat, source_lon,\n        stations_df['latitude'].values,\n        stations_df['longitude'].values\n    )\n    return distances\n```\n\n## Travel Time Calculation\n\n```python\ndef calculate_travel_times(distance_km, phase='P', vp=6.0, vs=3.43):\n    \"\"\"\n    Calculate seismic wave travel time.\n\n    Args:\n        distance_km: Distance in kilometers\n        phase: 'P' or 'S'\n        vp: P-wave velocity in km/s\n        vs: S-wave velocity in km/s\n\n    Returns:\n        Travel time in seconds\n    \"\"\"\n    v = vp if phase == 'P' else vs\n    return distance_km / v\n```\n\n## Complete Example\n\n```python\nimport pandas as pd\nfrom datetime import datetime, timedelta\n\n# Station data\nstations = pd.DataFrame({\n    'station': ['CCC', 'CLC', 'SRT'],\n    'latitude': [35.52495, 35.81574, 35.69235],\n    'longitude': [-117.36453, -117.59751, -117.75051]\n})\n\n# Hypothetical earthquake\neq_lat = 35.7\neq_lon = -117.5\neq_time = datetime(2019, 7, 4, 17, 33, 49)\n\n# Calculate distances and arrival times\nvp = 6.0\nvs = vp / 1.75\n\nfor _, sta in stations.iterrows():\n    dist = haversine_distance(eq_lat, eq_lon, sta['latitude'], sta['longitude'])\n\n    p_travel_time = dist / vp\n    s_travel_time = dist / vs\n\n    p_arrival = eq_time + timedelta(seconds=p_travel_time)\n    s_arrival = eq_time + timedelta(seconds=s_travel_time)\n\n    print(f\"Station {sta['station']}:\")\n    print(f\"  Distance: {dist:.1f} km\")\n    print(f\"  P arrival: {p_arrival}\")\n    print(f\"  S arrival: {s_arrival}\")\n    print(f\"  S-P time: {s_travel_time - p_travel_time:.1f} s\")\n```\n\n## Coordinate System Notes\n\n- Latitude: -90 to +90 (negative = South)\n- Longitude: -180 to +180 (negative = West)\n- For California data (like CI lat1\n    dlon = lon2 - lon1\n    a = sin(dlat/2)**2 + cos(lat1) * cos(lat2) * sin(dlon/2)**2\n    c = 2 * atan2(sqrt(a), sqrt(1-a))\n    return R * c\n\n# Reference point (centroid of stations)\nref_lat = stations_unique['latitude'].mean()\nref_lon = stations_unique['longitude'].mean()\nprint(f\"Reference point: ({ref_lat:.4f}, {ref_lon:.4f})\")\n\n# Add station coordinates to picks\npicks_with_coords = picks_df.merge(\n    stations_unique[['station', 'latitude', 'longitude']],\n    on='station',\n    how='left'\n)\n\n# Drop picks without station coords\npicks_with_coords = picks_with_coords.dropna(subset=['latitude', 'longitude'])\nprint(f\"Picks with coordinates: {len(picks_with_coords)}\")\n\n# Calculate estimated origin time for each pick\n# origin_time = pick_time - travel_time\n# travel_time = distance / velocity\n\ndef estimate_origin_time(row, ref_lat, ref_lon, vp, vs):\n    dist = haversine_distance(ref_lat, ref_lon, row['latitude'], row['longitude'])\n    v = vp if row['phase'] == 'P' else vs\n    travel_time = dist / v  # seconds\n    origin_time = row['time_utc'] -
